compute the AUC with the \u003ccode\u003ey\u003c/code\u003e variable as ground truth.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eUse the training dataset for that\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf your AUC is \u0026lt; 0.5, invert this variable by putting \"-\" in front\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e(e.g. \u003ccode\u003e-df_train['balance']\u003c/code\u003e)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAUC can go below 0.5 if the variable is negatively correlated with the target variable. You can change the direction of the correlation by negating this variable - then negative correlation becomes positive.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhich numerical variable (among the following 4) has the highest AUC?\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003elead_score\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003enumber_of_courses_viewed\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003einteraction_count\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eannual_income\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv Ignore thresholds at\nwhich both metrics are zero, and choose the threshold with the smallest\nabsolute difference between precision and recall.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.43\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.63\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.73\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.83\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eQuestion 4: F1 score\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-question-4-f1-score\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Question 4: F1 score\" href=\"#question-4-f1-score\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ePrecision and recall are conflicting - when one grows, the other goes down. That's why they are often combined into the F1 score - a metrics that takes into account both\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis is the formula for computing F1:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cmath-renderer class=\"js-display-math\" style=\"display: block\" data-run-id=\"9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205\"\u003e$$F_1 = 2 \\cdot \\cfrac{P \\cdot R}{P + R}$$\u003c/math-renderer\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhere \u003cmath-renderer class=\"js-inline-math\" style=\"display: inline-block\" data-run-id=\"9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205\"\u003e$P$\u003c/math-renderer\u003e is precision and \u003cmath-renderer class=\"js-inline-math\" style=\"display: inline-block\" data-run-id=\"9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205\"\u003e$R$\u003c/math-renderer\u003e is recall.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eLet's compute F1 for all thresholds from 0.0 to 1.0 with increment 0.01\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAt which threshold F1 is maximal?\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.21\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.41\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.61\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e0.81\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv Homework 4","","\u003e [!NOTE]","\u003e This homework uses the pinned 2026 lead-scoring release in the course","\u003e repository. The plan and report are available in `cohorts/2026/data/`.","","","In this homework, we will use the 2026 lead-scoring dataset. Download it from [here](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/course_lead_scoring_2026.csv).","","","In this dataset our desired target for classification task will be `converted` variable - has the client signed up to the platform or not. ","","### Data preparation","","* Check if the missing values are presented in the features.","* If there are missing values:","    * For categorical features, replace them with 'NA'","    * For numerical features, replace with with 0.0 ","","","Split the data into 3 parts with these exact calls:","","```python","df_full_train, df_test = train_test_split(df, test_size=0.2, random_state=1)","df_train, df_val = train_test_split(","    df_full_train, test_size=0.25, random_state=1",")","```","","","### Question 1: ROC AUC feature importance","","ROC AUC could also be used to evaluate feature importance of numerical variables. ","","Let's do that","","* For each numerical variable, use it as score (aka prediction) and compute the AUC with the `y` variable as ground truth.","* Use the training dataset for that","","","If your AUC is \u003c 0.5, invert this variable by putting \"-\" in front","","(e.g. `-df_train['balance']`)","","AUC can go below 0.5 if the variable is negatively correlated with the target variable. You can change the direction of the correlation by negating this variable - then negative correlation becomes positive.","","Which numerical variable (among the following 4) has the highest AUC?","","- `lead_score`","- `number_of_courses_viewed`","- `interaction_count`","- `annual_income`","","### Question 2: Training the model","","Apply one-hot-encoding using `DictVectorizer` and train the logistic regression with these parameters:","","```python","LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=1.0, max_iter=1000)","```","","What's the AUC of this model on the validation dataset? (round to 3 digits)","","- 0.532","- 0.632","- 0.732","- 0.832","","","### Question 3: Precision and Recall","","Now let's compute precision and recall for our model.","","* Evaluate the model on all thresholds from 0.0 to 1.0 with step 0.01","* For each threshold, compute precision and recall","* Plot them","","At which threshold precision and recall curves intersect? Ignore thresholds at","which both metrics are zero, and choose the threshold with the smallest","absolute difference between precision and recall.","","* 0.43","* 0.63","* 0.73","* 0.83","","","### Question 4: F1 score","","Precision and recall are conflicting - when one grows, the other goes down. That's why they are often combined into the F1 score - a metrics that takes into account both","","This is the formula for computing F1:","","$$F_1 = 2 \\cdot \\cfrac{P \\cdot R}{P + R}$$","","Where $P$ is precision and $R$ is recall.","","Let's compute F1 for all thresholds from 0.0 to 1.0 with increment 0.01","","At which threshold F1 is maximal?","","- 0.21","- 0.41","- 0.61","- 0.81","","","### Question 5: 5-Fold CV","","","Use the `KFold` class from Scikit-Learn to evaluate our model on 5 different folds:","","```","KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=1)","```","","* Iterate over different folds of `df_full_train`","* Split the data into train and validation","* Train the model on train with these parameters: `LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=1.0, max_iter=1000)`","* Use AUC to evaluate the model on validation","","How large is standard deviation of the scores across different folds?","","- 0.001","- 0.007","- 0.013","- 0.060","","","### Question 6: Hyperparameter Tuning","","Now let's use 5-Fold cross-validation to find the best parameter `C`","","* Iterate over the following `C` values: `[0.000001, 0.001, 1]`","* Initialize `KFold` with the same parameters as previously","* Use these parameters for the model: `LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=C, max_iter=1000)`","* Compute the mean ROC AUC as well as the std (round the mean and std to 3","  decimal digits).","","Which `C` leads to the best mean ROC AUC?","","- 0.000001","- 0.001","- 1","","If you have ties, select the score with the lowest std. If you still have ties, select the smallest `C`.","","## Learning in Public","","Share what you learned from this homework in a short post, screenshot, or","notebook excerpt. Use `#mlzoomcamp`, tag Alexey Grigorev or DataTalksClub, and","submit the post link with your homework. Don't share credentials, private","data, or other secrets.","","See the [learning in public guide](https://datatalks.club/docs/courses/zoomcamp-logistics/learning-in-public/) for more","ideas and examples.","","### Example post for LinkedIn","","```text","🚀 Completed Module 4 of Machine Learning Zoomcamp 2026!","","I evaluated a classifier with ROC AUC, precision, recall, and F1, then used","cross-validation to measure score variation and tune a parameter.","","My repo: \u003cLINK\u003e","","#mlzoomcamp @Alexey Grigorev","```","","### Example post for X","","```text","Evaluated a classifier in Module 4 of #mlzoomcamp.","","✅ Compared ROC AUC and threshold-based metrics","✅ Found a useful precision/recall trade-off","✅ Used 5-fold cross-validation for tuning","","My repo: \u003cLINK\u003e","```","","## Submit the results","","* Submit your results here: https://courses.datatalks.club/ml-zoomcamp-2026/homework/hw04","* The numerical options are calculated from the pinned 2026 release. Use the value that matches your calculation; do not choose a merely close entry-content container-lg" itemprop="text"><div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Homework 4</h2><a id="user-content-homework-4" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Homework 4" href="#homework-4"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-alert markdown-alert-note" dir="auto"><p class="markdown-alert-title" dir="auto"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-info mr-2" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M0 8a8 8 0 1 1 16 0A8 8 0 0 1 0 8Zm8-6.5a6.5 6.5 0 1 0 0 13 6.5 6.5 0 0 0 0-13ZM6.5 7.75A.75.75 0 0 1 7.25 7h1a.75.75 0 0 1 .75.75v2.75h.25a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-2a.75.75 0 0 1 0-1.5h.25v-2h-.25a.75.75 0 0 1-.75-.75ZM8 6a1 1 0 1 1 0-2 1 1 0 0 1 0 2Z"></path></svg>Note</p><p dir="auto">This homework uses the pinned 2026 lead-scoring release in the course
repository. The plan and report are available in <code>cohorts/2026/data/</code>.</p>
</div>
<p dir="auto">In this homework, we will use the 2026 lead-scoring dataset. Download it from <a href="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DataTalksClub/machine-learning-zoomcamp/main/cohorts/2026/data/course_lead_scoring_2026.csv" rel="nofollow">here</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">In this dataset our desired target for classification task will be <code>converted</code> variable - has the client signed up to the platform or not.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Data preparation</h3><a id="user-content-data-preparation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Data preparation" href="#data-preparation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Check if the missing values are presented in the features.</li>
<li>If there are missing values:
<ul dir="auto">
<li>For categorical features, replace them with 'NA'</li>
<li>For numerical features, replace with with 0.0</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">Split the data into 3 parts with these exact calls:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="df_full_train, df_test = train_test_split(df, test_size=0.2, random_state=1)
df_train, df_val = train_test_split(
    df_full_train, test_size=0.25, random_state=1
)"><pre><span class="pl-s1">df_full_train</span>, <span class="pl-s1">df_test</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">train_test_split</span>(<span class="pl-s1">df</span>, <span class="pl-s1">test_size</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0.2</span>, <span class="pl-s1">random_state</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">df_train</span>, <span class="pl-s1">df_val</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">train_test_split</span>(
    <span class="pl-s1">df_full_train</span>, <span class="pl-s1">test_size</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0.25</span>, <span class="pl-s1">random_state</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span>
)</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 1: ROC AUC feature importance</h3><a id="user-content-question-1-roc-auc-feature-importance" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 1: ROC AUC feature importance" href="#question-1-roc-auc-feature-importance"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">ROC AUC could also be used to evaluate feature importance of numerical variables.</p>
<p dir="auto">Let's do that</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>For each numerical variable, use it as score (aka prediction) and compute the AUC with the <code>y</code> variable as ground truth.</li>
<li>Use the training dataset for that</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">If your AUC is &lt; 0.5, invert this variable by putting "-" in front</p>
<p dir="auto">(e.g. <code>-df_train['balance']</code>)</p>
<p dir="auto">AUC can go below 0.5 if the variable is negatively correlated with the target variable. You can change the direction of the correlation by negating this variable - then negative correlation becomes positive.</p>
<p dir="auto">Which numerical variable (among the following 4) has the highest AUC?</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code>lead_score</code></li>
<li><code>number_of_courses_viewed</code></li>
<li><code>interaction_count</code></li>
<li><code>annual_income</code></li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 2: Training the model</h3><a id="user-content-question-2-training-the-model" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 2: Training the model" href="#question-2-training-the-model"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Apply one-hot-encoding using <code>DictVectorizer</code> and train the logistic regression with these parameters:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=1.0, max_iter=1000)"><pre><span class="pl-en">LogisticRegression</span>(<span class="pl-s1">solver</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">'liblinear'</span>, <span class="pl-c1">C</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1.0</span>, <span class="pl-s1">max_iter</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1000</span>)</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">What's the AUC of this model on the validation dataset? (round to 3 digits)</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.532</li>
<li>0.632</li>
<li>0.732</li>
<li>0.832</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 3: Precision and Recall</h3><a id="user-content-question-3-precision-and-recall" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 3: Precision and Recall" href="#question-3-precision-and-recall"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Now let's compute precision and recall for our model.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Evaluate the model on all thresholds from 0.0 to 1.0 with step 0.01</li>
<li>For each threshold, compute precision and recall</li>
<li>Plot them</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">At which threshold precision and recall curves intersect? Ignore thresholds at
which both metrics are zero, and choose the threshold with the smallest
absolute difference between precision and recall.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.43</li>
<li>0.63</li>
<li>0.73</li>
<li>0.83</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 4: F1 score</h3><a id="user-content-question-4-f1-score" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 4: F1 score" href="#question-4-f1-score"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Precision and recall are conflicting - when one grows, the other goes down. That's why they are often combined into the F1 score - a metrics that takes into account both</p>
<p dir="auto">This is the formula for computing F1:</p>
<p dir="auto"><math-renderer class="js-display-math" style="display: block" data-run-id="9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205">$$F_1 = 2 \cdot \cfrac{P \cdot R}{P + R}$$</math-renderer></p>
<p dir="auto">Where <math-renderer class="js-inline-math" style="display: inline-block" data-run-id="9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205">$P$</math-renderer> is precision and <math-renderer class="js-inline-math" style="display: inline-block" data-run-id="9e862dca152c23499d9ba0792bcaf205">$R$</math-renderer> is recall.</p>
<p dir="auto">Let's compute F1 for all thresholds from 0.0 to 1.0 with increment 0.01</p>
<p dir="auto">At which threshold F1 is maximal?</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.21</li>
<li>0.41</li>
<li>0.61</li>
<li>0.81</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 5: 5-Fold CV</h3><a id="user-content-question-5-5-fold-cv" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 5: 5-Fold CV" href="#question-5-5-fold-cv"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Use the <code>KFold</code> class from Scikit-Learn to evaluate our model on 5 different folds:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=1)"><pre class="notranslate"><code>KFold(n_splits=5, shuffle=True, random_state=1)
</code></pre></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Iterate over different folds of <code>df_full_train</code></li>
<li>Split the data into train and validation</li>
<li>Train the model on train with these parameters: <code>LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=1.0, max_iter=1000)</code></li>
<li>Use AUC to evaluate the model on validation</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">How large is standard deviation of the scores across different folds?</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.001</li>
<li>0.007</li>
<li>0.013</li>
<li>0.060</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question 6: Hyperparameter Tuning</h3><a id="user-content-question-6-hyperparameter-tuning" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question 6: Hyperparameter Tuning" href="#question-6-hyperparameter-tuning"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Now let's use 5-Fold cross-validation to find the best parameter <code>C</code></p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Iterate over the following <code>C</code> values: <code>[0.000001, 0.001, 1]</code></li>
<li>Initialize <code>KFold</code> with the same parameters as previously</li>
<li>Use these parameters for the model: <code>LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear', C=C, max_iter=1000)</code></li>
<li>Compute the mean ROC AUC as well as the std (round the mean and std to 3
decimal digits).</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">Which <code>C</code> leads to the best mean ROC AUC?</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>0.000001</li>
<li>0.001</li>
<li>1</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">If you have ties, select the score with the lowest std. If you still have ties, select the smallest <code>C</code>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Learning in Public</h2><a id="user-content-learning-in-public" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Learning in Public" href="#learning-in-public"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
